# Audit: Apple HIG component pass (iOS) — 2026-08-29

**Overall working-tree verdict:** SOURCE READY WITH EXPLICIT GATES
**Audit type:** source, focused contract tests, local builds, and artifact review; authenticated simulator/device, deployment, and production proof remain separate
**Scope:** Wisconsin signed-in native app shell and representative shared surfaces, evaluated component-by-component against Apple's current Human Interface Guidelines.

This record describes the current working tree, which already contained unrelated user changes before the audit. The 2026-08-29 pass ledger below is retained as the baseline snapshot; the consolidated remediation ledger that follows is authoritative for the current checkout. `SOURCE READY` means the inspected source has no P0/P1 blocker for that component, but runtime proof remains open. `NOT READY` in the baseline ledger means that component had a current P0/P1 finding before this remediation pass.

## Consolidated remediation ledger — 2026-08-30

All 75 unchecked findings in the original source review have now been rechecked. Seventy-four are source-closed in this checkout and marked `[x]` below. P2-291 remains intentionally open as an accepted product/security exception under D-043; it is not silently treated as an implementation defect. The remaining unchecked items are runtime, deployment, or policy gates recorded in the closing section of this ledger rather than source findings.

| Group | Findings closed | Current source evidence | Remaining gate |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Navigation, modality, and keyboard | P1-210, 219–220; P2-282, 286–288, 292, 306, 308, 330–332 | Native navigation paths preserve destination identity, modal task boundaries use Close/Cancel/Back consistently, confirmation titles are compact, invited registration skips disabled fields, scene destinations restore, and Command-Comma reaches Settings. | iPhone 16 Pro interaction/accessibility acceptance; Mac UI acceptance is separately noted below. |
| Controls, layout, and menus | P1-217–218, 221–224, 236; P2-283–285, 294, 299–305, 309, 313–314, 329, 333–334 | Interactive targets use the 44-point floor, accessibility-size kiosk rosters collapse to one column, time input is bounded, lifecycle actions are visible, system Toggle/Picker controls own selection state, copy and context actions have visible alternatives, and custom haptic duplication is removed. | Dynamic Type, VoiceOver, keyboard, and physical kiosk inspection. |
| Account, privacy, and inclusive writing | P1-225; P2-289–290, 310, 315 | Self-delete now pseudonymizes retained custody/audit attribution, removes direct identity/authentication records, revokes credentials and companion access, records the retention boundary, and uses capability-neutral permission/passkey and person-centered copy. | Authenticated production read-back; P2-291 is retained as the D-043 password-fallback exception. |
| Scanner, kiosk, imagery, and full screen | P1-235, 237; P2-271, 293, 295–297, 307, 325–327 | Scanner/photo surfaces keep readable system chrome, kiosk text editing accepts normal UIKit input, full-screen status access remains discoverable, loading/retry/thumbnail states are named, bundled imagery is profiled, and photo zoom has controls, retry, VoiceOver adjustment, and Reduce Motion behavior. Kiosk landscape enforcement remains the current iOS platform contract. | Managed kiosk, camera, VoiceOver, and physical-device acceptance; P2-307 remains a documented platform deadline. |
| Feedback, notifications, and discovery | P2-241–246, 263–264, 316–324 | Adaptive contrast, Reduce Motion, skeleton/toast announcements, valid symbols, mark-all/crop undo, notification timing/urgency/foreground behavior, snooze reconciliation, category actions/previews, TipKit cadence/eligibility, and chart selection marks are source-closed. | APNs, permission timing, VoiceOver, and live notification delivery proof. |
| State, refresh, and system shell | P2-298, 300–302, 311–312, 328 | Latest-request ownership prevents stale refreshes from publishing, unavailable commands hide, operational text is selectable, launch artwork is not used as a system launch frame, accent usage routes through Wisconsin tokens, and the calendar uses locale direction/weekday ordering. | Authenticated runtime and RTL/device rendering. |

**Accepted exception:** P2-291 is governed by [D-043](../docs/DECISIONS.md#d-043-passkeys-are-an-additive-sign-in-method-for-invite-granted-users). Passkeys are discoverable and phishing-resistant, but password sign-in and recovery intentionally remain available during rollout. Making a second factor mandatory or removing password recovery requires a separate accepted factor/recovery decision.

**Verification boundary:** Local source contracts, TypeScript, iOS project checks, drift checks, and the mandated iPhone 16 Pro iOS 26.5 Simulator build are evidence for the local checkout only. They do not claim authenticated production, APNs, VoiceOver, camera, managed-kiosk, Mac, iPad, physical-device, or deployment acceptance. The single consolidated before/after review artifact is `tasks/ios-hig-consolidated-review-2026-08-30/review.html`.

## Live index reconciliation — 2026-08-29

Apple's rendered HIG navigation, all six section pages, and all eight Components category pages were read directly. Category indexes were excluded from the standalone-page count; their leaf guidance pages were included. The first complete comparison found 157 current standalone pages, 120 represented by passes 1–127, and 37 missing. Passes 128–164 close all thirty-seven missing pages.

| Section | Current standalone pages | Covered before pass 128 | Covered now | Remaining |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Getting started | 8 | 0 | 8 | 0 |
| Foundations | 18 | 18 | 18 | 0 |
| Patterns | 25 | 25 | 25 | 0 |
| Components | 64 | 37 | 64 | 0 |
| Inputs | 13 | 11 | 13 | 0 |
| Technologies | 29 | 29 | 29 | 0 |
| **Total** | **157** | **120** | **157** | **0** |

The remaining live queue is empty. Every current standalone page in Apple's rendered index has a logged disposition.

Historical and intentional extras are reconciled as follows:

- Pass 6 points to the live **Menus and actions** category index and was labeled “Menus”; it does not cover the current standalone **Menus** leaf, which remains in the queue.
- Pass 19 **Badging** is retained as historical evidence; Apple now returns a not-found page and no current section lists it.
- Passes 24 Refresh content controls, 34 Radio buttons, 37 Navigation bars, 53 Image buttons, and 83 Requesting permission intentionally audit named subsections of current standalone pages. They are supplementary passes, not duplicate standalone-page dispositions.

## Findings

### P1 — address before a HIG-ready release

- [x] **Tab bar disappears inside a pushed guide reader.** A guide article explicitly hides the tab bar even though it remains within top-level navigation. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/GuidesView.swift:359`.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): Guide Reader no longer hides the system tab bar, so the pushed article remains inside the top-level navigation shell and preserves the normal escape route.
- [x] **The iOS 26.0 tab shell accepts a visible blank accessory pill.** The compatibility branch keeps `tabViewBottomAccessory` attached with empty content to avoid rebuilding navigation state; the source comment documents the artifact. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/AppTabView.swift:332-353`.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): the iOS 26.0 compatibility branch omits the bottom accessory when no reservation draft is active; iOS 26.1 uses `isEnabled`, so the shell never renders an inactive empty pill.
- [x] **Reservation wizard Back occupies the cancellation slot as text.** Steps two and three replace Cancel with a text-only Back control instead of preserving clear modal cancellation and standard navigation semantics. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBookingSheet.swift:236-245`.
- [x] **Three pushed detail destinations suppress their navigation identity.** Guide Reader, Booking Detail, and Item Detail all set an empty navigation title (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/GuidesView.swift:359-361`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/BookingDetailView.swift:137-138`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemDetailView.swift:87-88`). Guide Reader's and Item Detail's in-content titles are not headings (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/GuidesView.swift:440-452`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemDetailView.swift:503-509`); Booking Detail adds a heading trait to its title but then replaces all child accessibility semantics with one combined label (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/BookingDetailView.swift:721-739`). VoiceOver therefore has no system destination title to announce after the push and no equivalent first heading on these screens. Supply a concise navigation title, even if its visual treatment remains inline, or preserve an equivalent heading as the first destination identity.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): Guide Reader, Booking Detail, and Item Detail now provide concise data-backed navigation titles; the inline Guide and Item titles are headings, and Booking Detail contains its section children so VoiceOver can traverse the detail hierarchy instead of receiving one opaque combined label.
- [x] **Narrowing the regular-width sidebar discards the active destination.** Scoreboard, Guides, Licenses, and Users exist in the tab shell only while the horizontal size class is regular (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AppTabView.swift:25-26`, `:84-119`). If any of those sidebar-only values is selected when the iPad window narrows or the view reappears compact, the shell explicitly changes the selection to Home (`:41-42`, `:122-130`) instead of collapsing to the same logical destination or its Browse route. This loses the person's place during normal iPad resizing, and the focused test currently requires the reset (`tests/ios-tabbar-stability.test.ts:71-78`). Preserve the selected destination through compact collapse, or translate it into Browse's navigation stack and restore it when regular width returns.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): the shell records the selected sidebar destination, routes compact mode through Browse's native path, and restores the same sidebar tab when regular width returns without forcing Home.
- [x] **Several frequent reservation controls and the photo viewer's image-only close button are below the 44-point interaction baseline.** Draft dismissal is 28 points (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/ReservationDraftCard.swift:48-53`); selected-equipment steppers are 36 points (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBooking/CreateBookingEquipmentRows.swift:193-220`); picker steppers, category chips, and battery recommendation controls are 28–34 points (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBooking/CreateBookingEquipmentPicker.swift:579-606`, `:618-630`, `:650-681`); and the photo viewer close button is 38 points (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Brand.swift:463-472`). This is one shared repair item across the Buttons and Image buttons passes.
- [x] **Schedule calendar days intentionally use a 32-point target.** The source records the layout tradeoff, but the interactive cell remains below the general 44-point guidance. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:2212-2219`.
- [x] **Modal tasks layer and branch into additional modal experiences instead of completing or replacing the current one.** Edit Booking presents Transfer above itself (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/BookingDetailView.swift:462-490`), Trade Board presents Post Trade (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Schedule/TradeBoardSheet.swift:348-399`), and the full-screen scanner presents manual entry as another sheet (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/QRScannerSheet.swift:51-70`). Profile is the most app-like example: Home presents it as a sheet (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/HomeView.swift:493-496`), but that sheet contains a branched NavigationStack into Settings, bookings, shifts, availability, notification/account controls, and other destinations, then layers push and badge sheets above the hierarchy (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ProfileView.swift:46-104`). Collapse related subtasks into a single modal path, dismiss before routing into a different app destination, and make Profile a normal app destination rather than a miniature app inside a sheet.
- [x] **Several modal surfaces provide a lone Done dismissal.** Notifications uses Done in the cancellation position (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NotificationsSheet.swift:143-156`); Trade Board and Profile likewise expose Done without a distinct Cancel, Close, or Back alternative (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Schedule/TradeBoardSheet.swift:348-386`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ProfileView.swift:64-73`).
- [x] **The kiosk identity collection does not preserve the idle roster's accessibility-size layout.** The idle collection detects an accessibility Dynamic Type size and switches to one comfortable scrolling column (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskIdleView.swift:529-539`), but the identity picker calls `KioskRosterMetrics.resolve` directly with no Dynamic Type environment or fallback (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskIdentityView.swift:116-136`). It can therefore keep a dense multi-column, single-line, scale-down grid on the screen where a person must identify themself correctly.
- [x] **The Schedule calendar collection retains the existing 32-point day target finding.** Selection feedback and accessibility traits are present, but each peer item in the seven-column collection remains below the general interaction baseline. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:2166-2219`. This is the same underlying issue already counted in the Buttons pass, not a second repair item.
- [x] **Two quarter-hour time selectors put a full day into one menu.** Reservation scheduling creates 96 values and presents them with `.pickerStyle(.menu)` (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBookingSheet.swift:703`, `:746-768`); Add Shift does the same with 15-minute values (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Schedule/AddShiftSheet.swift:319-327`, `:331-338`). A 96-command menu is difficult to scan and navigate; use a compact time picker or bounded hour/minute selection that preserves the 15-minute contract.
- [x] **Registration keyboard progression targets a disabled field.** An invited email makes the Email field locked, but submitting Full name always assigns focus to that disabled Email field instead of Password. The primary invitation flow therefore breaks sequential keyboard navigation and forces a tap to continue. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/NativeAuthViews.swift:49-60`, `:72-91`.
- [x] **“Delete Account” permanently deactivates access but does not delete the account or its personal authentication/profile data.** Native copy promises “Permanently delete this account?” and “This cannot be undone from the app” (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AccountSecuritySettingsView.swift:536-590`), but the route explicitly delegates to `deactivateUserWithCleanup` and audits only `active: true` → `false` (`src/app/api/me/account/route.ts:14-49`). That service deletes sessions and reset tokens, revokes notification access, cancels eligible future work, and then updates only `User.active` (`src/lib/services/user-deactivation.ts:169-297`); it does not erase or pseudonymize the retained name, unique email, password hash, phone/Wiscard/profile fields, avatar URL, passkey credentials, or account relations declared on `User` (`prisma/schema.prisma:201-245`, `:359-374`). The focused route test codifies deactivation as successful “deletion” (`tests/me-account-deletion.test.ts:37-52`). Apple requires an in-app-created account to support deletion, not merely deactivation, while permitting clearly explained retention only where operational or legal requirements require specific records. Define the minimum historical custody/audit retention contract, erase or irreversibly pseudonymize the remaining account and authentication data, revoke every credential, and report a truthful completion or scheduled status instead of presenting deactivation as permanent deletion.
- [x] **Native notification Settings can report delivery as enabled while account or device delivery is unavailable.** `pausedUntil` is a durable account preference and suppresses both push and email while it remains in the future (`src/lib/services/notification-prefs.ts:21-25`, `:87-100`). The native view model parses that value and already implements pause, resume, and `isPaused` (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Preferences.swift:111-137`), but `NotificationSettingsView` never reads any of them: its status is derived only from the push-channel toggle, its type toggles remain enabled, and its device-specific test bypasses the normal preference gate (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NotificationSettingsView.swift:18-104`, `:297-350`; `src/app/api/devices/test/route.ts:13-41`). The root hub similarly labels Notifications “Push” from only the server channel and OS authorization, without considering pause state, a missing installation token, or failed APNs registration (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/SettingsView.swift:32-36`, `:170-177`). An account paused on the web or a device whose registration failed can therefore appear enabled, and a deliberate test can succeed while ordinary alerts remain suppressed. Surface the active pause and its end time, provide Resume (and equivalent pause choices if this is a cross-platform account setting), disable or clearly qualify conflicting controls, and distinguish OS authorization, APNs registration, account delivery policy, and deliberate test delivery. **Resolved 2026-08-29:** Native Settings now renders the canonical pause with end time and Resume/Pause choices, qualifies the controls, reports iOS/APNs/account state separately, and gates the device self-test on ordinary push eligibility.
- [x] **A failed notification preference change is reverted without visible or spoken recovery feedback.** `NotificationPrefsViewModel.save` restores the previous preferences and stores a concrete error when the update fails (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Preferences.swift:153-166`), but `NotificationSettingsView` renders `prefsVM.error` only when `prefsVM.prefs` is nil (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NotificationSettingsView.swift:37-56`, `:297-319`). A normal toggle save starts with loaded preferences, so the control silently flips back and the error remains hidden; the only failure cue is an app-authored haptic. Show and announce the save error while keeping the revert, clear stale errors before the next attempt, and provide an appropriate retry path without claiming the new preference persisted. **Resolved 2026-08-29:** Loaded save failures render an inline Reload action, are announced through VoiceOver, and clear before the next mutation while preserving the revert.
- [x] **Schedule's private working copy has only an all-or-nothing Revert, not single-action undo and redo.** Staff can add, assign, convert, unassign, remove, duplicate, and retime slots through version-checked working-copy commands (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/APIClient.swift:1718-1833`; `src/app/api/shift-groups/[id]/working-copy/route.ts:36-61`), but each successful command overwrites the one current payload at the next version (`src/lib/services/schedule-working-copy.ts:328-347`, `:652-706`). Event Detail clearly summarizes pending changes and provides a confirmed “Revert pending changes” action (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:390-427`, `:805-812`, `:1276-1315`), yet that action deletes the entire working copy (`src/lib/services/schedule-working-copy.ts:894-926`). After several edits, correcting the last mistake therefore discards every pending change; ended-event corrections publish immediately and have no equivalent recovery. **Resolved 2026-08-30:** the private working copy now stores bounded actor-owned before/after snapshots, moves named commands between Undo and Redo stacks through a serializable expected-version transaction, clears redo on a new edit, rejects stale or another operator's inverse action, and returns the resulting state. Native Event Detail exposes retryable feedback, visible Undo/Redo buttons, Command-Z/Shift-Command-Z, and the system UndoManager route while retaining the confirmed batch Revert. Local service/schema/route/native contracts and the iPhone 16 Pro iOS 26.5 Simulator build pass; migration deployment, authenticated runtime, and physical-device gesture proof remain open.
- [x] **The static Action button catalog can offer a role-inapplicable shortcut that silently does nothing.** The six `AppShortcut` entries are registered for every installation (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppIntents.swift:94-158`), unlike the Home Screen quick-action menu, which deliberately filters Scan, My Gear, Schedule, and Reserve Gear from the signed-in collaborator's capabilities (`ios/Wisconsin/App/QuickActions.swift:105-121`). When a static shortcut reaches the app, `routePendingAppIntent()` declines a missing capability without feedback; Schedule clears the request immediately, while Scan, My Gear, and Reserve can leave the pending request parked (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AppTabView.swift:290-302`). Even a collaborator allowed into gear search cannot use Scan: the destination view consumes the request and returns without opening the scanner (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/GlobalSearchSheet.swift:162-169`). A person can therefore configure the hardware button successfully and then receive no visible, spoken, or haptic result. Keep only universally meaningful App Shortcuts, or make each intent resolve the signed-in capability and return a clear unavailable/sign-in result; never leave a denied destination pending.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): each static intent checks the signed-in capability before creating its handoff and throws a localized sign-in/unavailable result when it cannot run. In-app routing also clears denied destinations and announces a visible recovery message; allowed collaborators now reach the scanner rather than consuming the request silently.
- [x] **The “What’s Out” result can overflow a snippet’s 400-point custom-view limit.** The intent requests and renders up to ten checkouts (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppIntentsData.swift:33-43`, `:77`), and each row contains a title, item summary and due-state line plus inter-row spacing and outer padding (`:115-153`). At the declared text styles this intrinsic stack already exceeds 400 points before larger Dynamic Type sizes, while both title and item lines are forced to one line. The snippet has no bounded summary or button/deep link to the complete My Gear destination. Limit the custom view to the few most urgent rows or a compact aggregate that fits at accessibility sizes, state how many remain, and provide a route to the full list instead of depending on clipping or truncation.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): the interactive snippet renders at most three urgent rows, states the remaining count, removes visual dialog duplication, and provides Open My Gear.
- [x] **Black full-screen scanner and photo surfaces do not establish a readable status-bar appearance or temporarily hide it.** The main scene lets a person force Light appearance (`ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:14-39`), but the authorized scanner and full-screen image viewer both place black content beneath the transparent status-bar region without a dark color-scheme override, status-bar style owner, or temporary hide/show treatment (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/QRScannerSheet.swift:45-59`, `:104-134`; `ios/Wisconsin/Core/Brand.swift:422-475`). In Light mode the inherited dark status indicators can therefore lose contrast against the black camera or photo background; the viewer also keeps the bar continuously visible during the immersive media task. Give both presentations an explicit readable system appearance, or hide the status bar while the black surface is active and restore it on dismissal; for the photo viewer, a single discoverable tap may toggle chrome if immersive viewing remains the goal.
- [x] **Crew-row lifecycle commands are available only through an overloaded long-press menu.** Open-slot assignment and pending-request approval/decline have visible row controls, but Replace, Trade Board posting/removal, Change Call Time, Duplicate Shift, Remove, and Delete Shift live only in `rowContextMenu` (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:1620-1629`, `:1760-1877`, `:1905-1973`). The hidden menu can also grow across four groups with one action per assignment, despite Apple's guidance to keep context menus small and to make every command available in the main interface. Move the frequent and consequential commands into a visible row overflow or dedicated shift-detail surface, then keep long press as a shortcut to the same bounded set.
- [x] **Kiosk title fields can reject standard Paste, text drag/drop, and Scribble transcription as scanner bursts.** `KioskNativeTextField` is a `UITextField`, which provides built-in text editing and Scribble, but its delegate sends every user-driven replacement through `KioskHIDBurstDetector`; any replacement string of six or more characters, or six characters arriving within 0.35 seconds, is rejected and the old value restored (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskNativeTextField.swift:12-26`, `:151-171`). This blocks the edit menu's Paste command and moving or dropping an ordinary selected phrase, and it can also discard a six-character-or-longer word or phrase when iPadOS delivers Scribble's recognized text as a multi-character replacement. The affected fields are the active-checkout title (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskCheckoutDetailSheet.swift:333-343`) and checkout booking name (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskCheckoutView.swift:1461-1479`). The first rejection has no visible explanation; the second reports “Finish editing before scanning,” even though the action was ordinary text entry. Route HID scanner detection outside the text-field delegate, or otherwise distinguish actual scanner events from UIKit editing transactions, so standard editing and Scribble remain intact while scanner input is still kept out of the field. This one repair item is shared by the Edit menus, Drag and drop, and Apple Pencil and Scribble passes.
- [x] **Home Screen quick actions are handled only by a callback that scene-based apps do not receive.** Wisconsin is a SwiftUI scene-based app with an `App` entry point and `WindowGroup` (`ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:7-24`), but its only shortcut entry point is the deprecated `UIApplicationDelegate.application(_:performActionFor:completionHandler:)` callback (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppDelegate.swift:23-36`). Apple states that this method is not called for scene-based apps and requires `UIWindowSceneDelegate.windowScene(_:performActionFor:completionHandler:)`, plus `UIScene.ConnectionOptions.shortcutItem` handling at cold launch; no corresponding scene handler exists in the target. As a result, the four polished menu items can appear but have no supported warm- or cold-launch route. The focused source test currently asserts the obsolete callback instead of detecting this break (`tests/ios-quick-actions-spotlight.test.ts:53-68`).

  Resolution (2026-08-29): AppDelegate now installs GearTrackerSceneDelegate through configurationForConnecting; the scene delegate handles warm actions and cold `connectionOptions.shortcutItem`, both using the existing handoff to the SwiftUI shell.
- [x] **Explicit white labels and glyphs lose required contrast on the custom adaptive fills in Dark Mode.** `brandPrimary` and every `statusText` tone deliberately brighten when the interface becomes dark, but the providers branch only on `userInterfaceStyle`, with no Increase Contrast variant (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Brand.swift:7-17`, `:156-187`). Several controls then force white over those bright values: the Blast banner's small “Got it” action paints `Color.statusText(tone)` behind white (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/BlastBanner.swift:47-55`), and reservation quantity controls put white plus glyphs on the dark-mode purple (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBooking/CreateBookingEquipmentRows.swift:210-216`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBooking/CreateBookingEquipmentPicker.swift:664-670`). Calculation from the declared sRGB values puts white against the dark status fills at about 1.8–2.9:1, including 2.58:1 for purple; this is below Apple's contrast guidance for the small text and below the 3:1 graphical-control threshold for the plus glyphs. Choose an adaptive foreground (dark over bright Dark Mode fills), darken the relevant control fills, and provide an increased-contrast response. This single blocker is shared by the Color and Dark Mode passes.
- [x] **Automatic feedback banners in common tasks translate vertically even when Reduce Motion is enabled.** Home's operational blast stack always inserts each banner from the top with a snappy animation and declares no Reduce Motion environment (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/BlastBanner.swift:86-106`). The native scanner always moves lookup feedback up from the bottom (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/QRScannerSheet.swift:102-116`), and the kiosk operator hub always moves scan feedback down from the top through unconditional `withAnimation` calls (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskOperatorHubView.swift:47-65`, `:660-674`); neither view reads `accessibilityReduceMotion`. These are automatic, repeated y-axis transitions in dashboard and scan workflows, which Apple specifically recommends replacing with fades under Reduce Motion. Preserve the existing text, haptic, and spoken feedback, but select opacity-only transitions and remove the explicit spatial animation when the system preference is on.
- [x] **Primary skeleton screens hide every placeholder from VoiceOver without exposing an equivalent loading status.** The shared skeleton shape is correctly hidden as decorative (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/Skeleton.swift:5-22`), but Home composes its entire initial dashboard from those hidden shapes with no accessible status (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/HomeView.swift:175-194`). Items, Bookings, Schedule, and Users go further and hide the whole initial placeholder list (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:311-324`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/BookingsView.swift:465-478`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:985-998`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/UsersView.swift:173-186`). On a slow first fetch, VoiceOver therefore encounters navigation chrome and no explanation that content is still arriving, even though sighted users receive a clear skeleton. Keep the decorative shapes hidden, but give each screen one concise live loading element or container label and verify its announcement and replacement when content or an error arrives.
- [x] **Shared transient toasts disappear without announcing their success or error to VoiceOver.** `ToastModifier` renders feedback as static overlay text, waits 2.5 seconds, and removes it, but never posts an accessibility announcement or moves screen-reader focus (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/Toast.swift:25-55`, `:58-83`). This is the only confirmation for reservation-draft save/failure (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AppTabView.swift:207-216`), item favorite failures (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:476-481`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemDetailView.swift:187-213`), and several Schedule follow, mute, routing, trade, and claim outcomes (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:466-475`, `:499-503`, `:1240-1256`). A VoiceOver user can therefore miss material action feedback before it vanishes. Announce each new message and keep the existing reduced-motion behavior; if the message requires a response, use a persistent surface instead of extending the timer.
- [x] **Five requested SF Symbol names do not exist in the iOS 26 catalog, leaving blank glyphs in six native states.** Camera-denied recovery requests `camera.slash` in both the main scanner and sticker wizard (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScanPrePromptView.swift:98-100`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/DevTools/LinkStickerWizard.swift:141-144`); the reservation-location warning requests `mappin.slash.fill` (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBooking/CreateBookingEquipmentPicker.swift:749-754`); Scoreboard failure requests `trophy.slash` (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScoreboardView.swift:1122-1125`); disconnected kiosk scanner status requests `barcode.viewfinder.slash` (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskFlowRouting.swift:84-90`); and two earned badges map Clapperboard to `clapperboard.fill` instead of the catalog's `movieclapper` family (`ios/Wisconsin/Shared/BadgeEarnedCelebration.swift:193-200`). Exact-name searches in the installed SF Symbols catalog still return no iOS 26 record for any of the five names. Replace them with available, semantically equivalent symbols and add catalog-backed availability coverage so future dynamic mappings cannot silently render empty. This is one shared blocker across the Icons and SF Symbols passes.
- [x] **The native profile crop produces device-scaled pixels instead of the declared 1024-pixel upload.** `croppedJPEGData` creates a `UIGraphicsImageRenderer` with a 1024-by-1024 point size but does not override its format scale (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/ThumbnailLoader.swift:54-59`). UIKit's default renderer scale equals the main screen scale, so the default iPhone 16 Pro path creates a 3072-by-3072-pixel JPEG, not 1024 by 1024. The source-contract test only searches for the point dimensions and therefore endorses the defect (`tests/ios-native-welcome.test.ts:81-95`). That unnecessarily triples each dimension, increases decode/upload work, and can push a detailed quality-0.9 photo through the server's hard 4.5 MB image boundary (`src/lib/blob.ts:5-12`, `:45-55`), turning a valid crop into the generic save failure. Use an explicit renderer format with scale 1 (and an intentional color space), encode directly from that renderer, and test the output pixel dimensions plus maximum payload behavior.
- [x] **The main app uses a required-reason file-timestamp API without declaring it in its privacy manifest.** MetricKit report pruning requests `creationDateKey` and reads each report's creation date (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/PerformanceInstrumentation.swift:275-285`), an API Apple lists under `NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryFileTimestamp`. The bundled main-app manifest declares only `NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryUserDefaults` (`ios/Wisconsin/Supporting/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:5-15`), and its focused contract validates collected-data categories but never checks the main target's accessed-API declarations (`tests/ios-privacy-manifest.test.ts:26-60`). Apple states that App Store Connect does not accept app-owned required-reason API use without a matching manifest entry. Declare the file-timestamp category with the container-metadata reason `C617.1`, and extend the contract to keep the compiled MetricKit source and manifest in sync.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): the main-app manifest declares `NSPrivacyAccessedAPICategoryFileTimestamp` with reason `C617.1`, and `tests/ios-privacy-manifest.test.ts` derives its expectation from the `.creationDateKey` calls in the compiled MetricKit source. The kiosk manifest remains target-scoped.
- [x] **Primary global Search silently hides valid matches after the first ten from each source.** `SearchService` requests `limit: 10` for assets, reservations, check-outs, and people, then reduces each API response to its first-page arrays and discards the server-provided totals (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/SearchService.swift:83-89`, `:95-103`, `:123-130`). The Search UI labels each category with only the returned array count and offers no pagination, “View all,” or route into the corresponding complete local result set (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/GlobalSearchSheet.swift:329-365`). Because Search is the dedicated primary discovery location, a person can enter an exact valid term, see ten plausible matches, and have no indication that the desired eleventh match exists. Preserve total-result truth and provide bounded pagination or a clearly labeled path to all matching results for every searchable source.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): Search now retains each endpoint's total and cursor, labels sections with loaded/total counts, loads more per source with independent retry, and preserves successful source groups during partial failures. The first-page and expanded-result states are captured in the consolidated UI review artifact.
- [x] **Bulk-item family matches are dead ends in global Search, including after a successful scan.** A family scan replaces the query/results with one family match (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/GlobalSearchSheet.swift:125-133`), but typed and scanned family results render as bare `ItemFamilyResultRow` values with no button, navigation, context menu, or reservation action (`:329-334`). The ordinary Items list already supports Reserve from the same family model through swipe and context-menu actions (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:292-297`, `:453-471`). Give the global result an obvious destination or visible Reserve action, preserving the scan as a completed path rather than closing the camera onto a noninteractive card.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): typed and scanned family rows are now actionable buttons with visible Reserve context and a direct reservation prefill, so a scan completes into an operational next step.
- [x] **Both operational widgets deliberately hide their useful content on locked surfaces even though this product data is explicitly nonprivate.** Next Shift marks both its system-family and Lock Screen layouts privacy-sensitive, and My Gear does the same to its entire card (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/GearHomeScreenWidgets.swift:177-204`, `:223-237`, `:280-311`). The focused test then requires that redaction as if it were a session-safety invariant (`tests/ios-home-screen-widgets.test.ts:94-101`). This can replace the shift, location, gear readiness, booking title and due state with a privacy treatment precisely where a widget is supposed to provide quick glanceable access. Remove the privacy-sensitive wall and update the stale source comments and contract while retaining account-switch cleanup so one account never inherits another account's cached snapshot.
- [x] **Widget timeline refreshes don't recompute the temporal state they claim changes at each boundary.** The provider schedules a new entry when a shift starts or ends and when a booking comes due, but each entry simply rereads the same cached snapshot (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/GearHomeScreenWidgets.swift:34-64`). That snapshot permanently stores the chosen next shift, each booking's `isOverdue`, and aggregate overdue/due-today counts at the last Home-dashboard load (`ios/Wisconsin/Widgets/GearWidgetPublisher.swift:28-56`), which is the only publication path (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/HomeView.swift:81-88`). Consequently, an ended shift can remain “Next Shift,” and a newly overdue booking can retain an “out” or “due today” headline and nonurgent color even while its relative timestamp moves into the past; the card waits 12 hours before admitting staleness. Recompute time-derived selection, counts and urgency for every timeline date, advance to the next cached shift where available, and use an appropriate app/background refresh path for server-owned changes instead of scheduling boundaries that preserve obsolete claims.

Resolution (2026-08-29): the widget and Live Activity source findings are closed in the native targets and source contracts. Widgets now publish non-sensitive operational content without `.privacySensitive()`, recompute cached temporal state at timeline dates, preserve bounded upcoming shifts, deep-link to exact event/booking identities with broad fallbacks, use verb-led descriptions, adapt colors and accent groups to system rendering modes, and provide compact background-removed compositions. The shared Live Activity `.small` family is documented and contract-tested for Apple Watch Smart Stack and CarPlay, including reduced-luminance handling. Simulator/source proof is complete; the Watch, CarPlay, Always-On, StandBy, physical-device, and distribution gates below remain open until exercised on their representative environments.

### P2 — worthwhile non-blocking improvements

- [x] **Mark All Read cannot be reversed after the server accepts it.** The inbox snapshots and restores its prior state only when `markAllNotificationsRead()` fails; after success every notification remains read (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NotificationsSheet.swift:67-79`, `:143-155`). The client API exposes `mark_read` and `mark_all_read` but no mark-unread operation (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/APIClient.swift:1558-1570`). Provide a brief, named Undo or a durable Mark Unread route so this routine bulk action is recoverable.
- [x] **Reset crop erases every current adjustment with no way to restore the pre-reset crop.** The custom crop editor combines drag, magnification, position sliders, and zoom, then applies “Reset crop” immediately (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Welcome/ProfilePhotoCropView.swift:20-70`, `:103-194`). Cancel can discard the entire editor and Save can commit it, but neither helps someone who taps Reset after several useful adjustments. Preserve at least the pre-reset state as a named one-step Undo; a deep history is unnecessary for this bounded editor.
- [x] **Four Action button labels don't begin with a verb.** Apple displays App Shortcut `shortTitle` values in the Action button configuration UI and asks for short, title-style, present-tense, verb-led labels. “My Gear,” “Schedule,” “What's Out,” and “Next Shift” are concise and understandable, but they're noun or question fragments; only “Scan Code” and “Reserve Gear” follow the full convention (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppIntents.swift:96-156`). Rename them with equally brief actions such as “Show Gear,” “View Schedule,” “Check Gear,” and “Check Shift.”

  Resolution (2026-08-29): the four labels now read Show Gear, View Schedule, Check Gear, and Check Shift.
- [x] **Both result snippets leave the spoken dialogue in the default visual position above custom content that communicates the same purpose.** The intents return `.result(dialog:view:)` for empty and populated states without selecting a dialogue-free visual treatment (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppIntentsData.swift:48-77`, `:179-199`). This is especially repetitive for Next Shift, where event, time and location appear in both the dialogue and custom view. Preserve complete standalone spoken dialogue for Siri, but omit it from the visual snippet and let the concise custom view communicate the result, as Apple recommends.

  Resolution (2026-08-29): both background intents now return an iOS 26 `SnippetIntent` alongside their complete spoken dialog. The snippet is rendered separately, caps checked-out rows at three with a “more in My Gear” summary, and offers Open My Gear/Open Schedule actions.
- [x] **The dedicated kiosk permanently hides the status bar with no reveal path.** `KioskShellView` applies both `.persistentSystemOverlays(.hidden)` and `.statusBarHidden()` to the entire app shell (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskShellView.swift:111-114`), and its screen padding is explicitly designed around that permanent state (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskChrome.swift:109-120`). The idle dashboard provides a large clock and connection-health treatment, which makes this reasonable appliance chrome, but active identity, checkout, pickup and return tasks provide no simple way to redisplay system time, battery or Wi-Fi state. Preserve the focused counter experience while offering an intentional system-status reveal or an equivalent persistent device-health treatment across active flows; verify the result in the actual managed-device mode.
- [x] **The paired-watch Live Activity bypasses the reduced-luminance palette even though its contract test claims otherwise.** `CheckoutReturnLockScreen` reads `isLuminanceReduced` and passes it into `ReturnPresentation`, but `CheckoutReturnSmallView` creates the same resolver without the environment value, so its Apple Watch Smart Stack tile always uses the full-intensity red, amber, and green accents (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/CheckoutReturnLiveActivityWidget.swift:242-254`, `:323-359`). The test's “always-on display drops to dimmed accents” assertion only finds one environment declaration and one dim color anywhere in the file, so it passes without proving the small-family path uses either (`tests/ios-checkout-return-live-activity-source.test.ts:81-88`). Thread reduced luminance through the small presentation, retain the stable layout and minute cadence, and scope the contract to the watch view so it can no longer endorse a disconnected implementation.
- [x] **The Live Activity's CarPlay family is mislabeled and absent from its source contract.** Apple uses the compact presentation for the default CarPlay Dashboard appearance and the supplemental `.small` family for a custom CarPlay layout, but the implementation comment says CarPlay uses `.medium` while both the adjacent comment and focused test describe `.small` as Apple Watch-only (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/CheckoutReturnLiveActivityWidget.swift:176-179`, `:224-239`, `:323-371`; `tests/ios-checkout-return-live-activity-source.test.ts:81-88`). The runtime path is fortunately the intended glanceable small layout, with one title, one countdown, one due/stale line, and no controls, but the false contract can let future watch-focused changes regress the vehicle surface unnoticed. Name CarPlay as a co-owner of the small family, add path-scoped assertions for its no-control, privacy-redacted, minute-cadence layout, and cover Apple's 240x78, 240x100, and 170x78 CarPlay sizes in simulator proof.
- [x] **Widget taps stop at broad list destinations even though each snapshot carries the exact record identity.** Next Shift sends every family to `wisconsin://schedule`, which opens today's general Schedule, and My Gear sends every state to `wisconsin://bookings`, which opens the broad gear list (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/GearHomeScreenWidgets.swift:154-175`, `:265-278`; `ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:64-76`). The snapshot already carries `Shift.id` and `DueBooking.id` (`ios/Wisconsin/Widgets/GearWidgetSnapshot.swift:14-31`). Route the whole-widget tap to the displayed shift or most urgent booking when one exists, retain the broad destination for empty/aggregate states, and keep capability checks and stale-record recovery intact.
- [x] **Both widget-gallery descriptions begin with noun phrases instead of the action verbs Apple asks people to scan.** “Your next assigned shift…” and “What you have checked out…” are concise and accurate, but neither starts with an action (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/GearHomeScreenWidgets.swift:241-248`, `:345-352`). Use verb-led descriptions such as “See your next assigned shift…” and “Check what you have out…” while retaining one description for each widget's grouped sizes.
- [x] **The widgets don't define meaningful accent groups or inspect the active rendering mode.** Their hierarchy relies on raw purple, red, orange and blue foreground styles, while source contains no `widgetAccentable`, `widgetRenderingMode`, or rendering-mode-specific asset treatment (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/GearHomeScreenWidgets.swift:5-12`, `:177-237`, `:258-341`). Text preserves the underlying meaning, so tinted, clear and vibrant appearances remain usable, but the system can flatten every status/header element into the primary group and discard the intended glance hierarchy. Identify a restrained accent group, preserve high contrast and noncolor meaning in accented and vibrant modes, and add preview coverage for light, dark, clear, tinted, Lock Screen and low-light StandBy appearances.
- [x] **The same dense small-card composition automatically ships into background-removed StandBy and CarPlay contexts without adapting for distance.** Both widgets support `.systemSmall`, which Apple uses for StandBy and CarPlay, but the source reads only `widgetFamily`; it never considers whether the system removed the container background (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/GearHomeScreenWidgets.swift:150-175`, `:177-220`, `:254-311`, `:348-352`). Next Shift can present an overline, a two-line title, two time lines and a two-line detail string, while My Gear can present an overline, count, booking, due line and stale footer—mostly in headline-through-caption2 text intended for a handheld card. Add a background-removed composition that prioritizes one large, glanceable fact, demotes or removes secondary lines, retains noncolor meaning and verifies the CarPlay safety-sized layouts rather than treating ordinary Home Screen source as sufficient proof.

- [x] **Search recents are role- and interaction-dependent.** Empty-query recents appear only for collaborators, while other users receive scanner guidance (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/GlobalSearchSheet.swift:56-65`); a query is added to recents only through explicit keyboard submission, not when a live result is opened (`:415-420`).

  Resolution (2026-08-29): every role receives the same empty-query Recent surface, while scanner guidance remains an additional action for non-collaborators; opening an asset, family, booking, or person records the active query just like pressing Search.
- [x] **Several menu commands miss the platform's title style or required ellipsis.** Event Detail's “Set Student call time” and “Revert pending changes” use sentence-style phrasing, while Set Student Call Time also opens another choice surface before it can complete (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:796-811`). Availability likewise offers “Add class schedule” and “Add day away” before opening their editors (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AvailabilityView.swift:59-76`), and its row-level Edit plus Item Detail's Edit Item also require more information before completion (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AvailabilityView.swift:462-470`; `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemDetailView.swift:104-119`). Use concise title-style labels and append an ellipsis to the commands that require another view, such as “Set Student Call Time…”, “Add Class Schedule…”, “Add Day Away…”, and “Edit…”.
- [x] **The Items status menu draws selection state manually.** Ordinary Buttons conditionally add checkmark images instead of exposing a native toggle or picker selection semantic; VoiceOver announcement remains runtime-uncertain. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:858-884`.
- [x] **Returning a shared license is styled as destructive.** The action is a normal, reversible shared-slot handoff but uses destructive roles in both the button and confirmation. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/LicensesView.swift:206-216`, `:392-398`.
- [x] **Schedule's month grid communicates each event's venue category through dot hue alone.** The legend names green Home, orange Away, and gray Other, but every event remains the same four-point circular mark inside a day (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:1958-1970`, `:2115-2129`, `:2196-2205`). The day-cell accessibility label reports only date, event count, and whether the day includes the person's shift, not the venue categories (`:2217-2235`). The selected day's agenda still exposes the events and venue details, so this is nonblocking; add a shape, symbol, or other non-hue distinction and include the visible venue summary in the day cell's spoken value.
- [x] **Reservation failure recovery uses the wrong modal choice surface and inconsistent action language.** An unexpected creation error appears through `confirmationDialog`, even though Apple's action-sheet guidance reserves that component for choices related to an intentional action and treats unexpected problems as alerts. It then pairs “Try again” with an `OK` cancel-role action instead of a clearer Retry/Cancel or Retry/Done alert. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBookingSheet.swift:138-153`.
- [x] **“Sign out other devices” is a staged command presented as a setting.** The switch defaults on and only takes effect when Change Password is submitted, so it does not represent an immediately applied persistent state like the app's other toggles. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/AccountSecuritySettingsView.swift:225-227`, `:497-521`. A clearly labeled option row or explicit post-change action would better communicate the one-time security consequence.
- [x] **Account setup hides its validation rules behind a disabled submission path.** `canSubmit` requires a syntactically valid email, an eight-character password, and matching confirmation, then disables Create Account until all conditions pass (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NativeAuthViews.swift:51-56`, `:115-129`). The form displays none of those requirements while typing; its specific validation messages are produced only by `submit()` (`:152-198`), which the disabled button cannot invoke. A person can discover them only by reaching Confirm password and pressing the keyboard's Done key. Keep progression unavailable until the required data is valid, but show concise inline email, password-length, and match feedback as values change so the disabled state is understandable.
- [x] **The required sign-in screen explains invitation mechanics but not why the account barrier benefits the person.** The scene says only “Sign in to your account,” while its footer says to enter an invited email or contact the operator (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/LoginView.swift:160-179`, `:475-480`). Requiring identity is justified because every useful workflow contains private schedules, reservations, custody, profile, or role-scoped operations, but Apple recommends saying that briefly on the sign-in view. Add one friendly outcome-focused line without implying public browsing is available.
- [x] **The passkey explainer names Face ID and Touch ID regardless of the current device's capabilities.** Account & Security always says “Use Face ID, Touch ID, or your device security” and native source performs no `LABiometryType` check (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AccountSecuritySettingsView.swift:55-58`). The actual command is correctly titled Add Passkey and Login correctly says Use a passkey; make the explanatory copy capability-neutral or resolve the one authentication method available in the current context.
- [ ] **Password-only authentication remains sufficient without a second factor.** Invited native onboarding requires a password and confirmation (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NativeAuthViews.swift:68-103`), and Login retains password as a complete authentication path alongside optional passkeys. No inspected native/server auth route or schema defines a verification-code, TOTP, or other second-factor step. Apple recommends passkey-first account creation when Sign in with Apple is not used and two-factor authentication when passwords remain available. Keep the invited identity boundary, but either make passkeys the normal enrollment path with a deliberate recovery strategy or add an appropriate second factor to password sign-in.
- [x] **The kiosk camera pairs Done with a close symbol and a cancellation callback.** The visible text implies successful completion while the `xmark` and `onCancel` semantics mean close/cancel. Use one consistent concept and label for the same action. `ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskBarcodeCameraView.swift:85-96`. This is the same semantic refinement counted by the Icons and SF Symbols passes.
- [x] **Several full-screen initial-loading indicators have no task label.** Notifications, Reports, and Overdue Report show only an indeterminate spinner while their first payload loads (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NotificationsSheet.swift:103-108`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ReportsView.swift:171-175`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/OverdueReportView.swift:36-40`). Add concise loading text or an accessibility label so the wait is identifiable rather than merely “in progress.”
- [x] **Photo-crop position sliders expose generic numeric values instead of directional meaning.** The sliders are labeled “Left or right” and “Up or down,” but their `-1...1` bindings have no minimum/maximum labels or explicit accessibility value, so VoiceOver receives a number without saying left, right, up, down, or centered (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Welcome/ProfilePhotoCropView.swift:153-181`, `:184-194`). Add concise endpoint cues and spoken directional values; the optional crop workflow remains usable through the system sliders, so this is non-blocking.
- [x] **Failed cached thumbnails collapse to blank tiles on two primary discovery surfaces.** `CachedThumbnail` renders only `Color.clear` until it has decoded an image and exposes neither a failure state nor HTTP-status validation (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/ThumbnailLoader.swift:122-165`). Items' `AssetThumbnail` and Search's bulk result thumbnail choose that loader whenever a URL parses, but put their bag/shipping-box placeholder only in the no-URL branch (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:790-815`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/SearchResultRow.swift:97-120`). A stale, denied, or malformed remote image therefore leaves a blank gray tile instead of the same meaningful fallback shown when no image exists. Keep the placeholder underneath the cached image, as the reservation thumbnail and avatar components already do, and validate successful HTTP responses before decoding.
- [x] **Bundled raster artwork has complete scale variants but no embedded color profile.** Badgers supplies proportional 72-, 144-, and 216-pixel PNGs and LaunchLockup supplies proportional 270×118, 540×236, and 810×354 PNGs through explicit 1x/2x/3x asset-catalog entries (`ios/Wisconsin/Assets.xcassets/Badgers.imageset/Contents.json:2-17`, `ios/Wisconsin/Assets.xcassets/LaunchLockup.imageset/Contents.json:2-17`), but metadata inspection reports no profile for any of those six files or the 1024-pixel legacy app-icon PNG. Apple recommends including a color profile with every image so brand color remains predictable across displays. Export the raster sources with an intentional sRGB or Display P3 profile and preserve the current pixel geometry.
- [x] **The full-screen item photo viewer drops image and recovery context.** The scan hero correctly wraps its image in a labeled Button before presenting the viewer (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/ScanResultHeroCard.swift:229-249`), but `ZoomableImageViewer` receives only a URL. Its successful image has no content label, its loading state is an unlabeled spinner, and its failure state is only an unlabeled symbol with no explanation or Retry (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Brand.swift:422-461`). VoiceOver therefore enters a full-screen modal that identifies only the Close button, and a temporary fetch failure gives every person less recovery information than the smaller source image. Pass a concise item-photo label into the viewer, identify loading and unavailable states, and provide a bounded retry without changing the existing fit, pinch, or close behavior.
- [x] **Several pull-to-refresh handlers can discard the user’s refresh while an older fetch is active.** Home and Notifications return immediately when already loading (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/HomeView.swift:66-70`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/NotificationsSheet.swift:15-17`); Event Detail and Trade Board do the same through their per-source fetching guards (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:50-54`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Schedule/TradeBoardSheet.swift:183-190`, `:225-228`). The system refresh indicator can therefore dismiss without issuing a fresh request. Use the established Guides/Licenses/Items/Bookings pattern in which an explicit refresh cancels or supersedes older work.
- [x] **Two copy commands exist only behind long press.** Items and search results expose Copy Asset Tag only in their context menus even though Item Detail's visible command copies the potentially different QR value (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:427-451`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/GlobalSearchSheet.swift:308-323`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemDetailView.swift:127-130`). Staff and admins can likewise see an unclaimed license code in a nonselectable row but can copy it only through the row's context menu (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/LicensesView.swift:410-415`, `:513-515`, `:555-560`, `:613-620`). Add the same copy commands to the corresponding detail or visible overflow interfaces.
- [x] **Schedule dims an unavailable context command instead of hiding it.** Follow/Mute remains in the event-row context menu with `.disabled(pendingFollowId != nil)` while any follow request is active (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:344-355`). Context menus are meant to omit currently unavailable actions; hide the command during the brief pending state or keep it actionable with per-event request ownership.
- [x] **Crew context-menu labels mix sentence style into a title-style command surface.** Labels such as “Assign someone,” “Claim this shift,” “Change call time,” “Duplicate shift,” and “Delete shift” conflict with Apple's title-style menu convention and with the capitalized multiword commands elsewhere in the app. `ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:1770-1876`.
- [x] **Useful static-text selection is inconsistent across equivalent layouts and operational identifiers.** Account Security enables the system edit menu for the account email only in the accessibility-size branch, not the regular layout (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AccountSecuritySettingsView.swift:320-349`). Item Details renders serial number and UW Asset Tag as monospaced, nonselectable values (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemDetailView.swift:559-573`, `:659-691`), leaving the already-recorded long-press Copy Asset Tag shortcut as the only copy route. Apply native text selection to content people may reasonably need to paste elsewhere, while keeping control labels nonselectable.
- [x] **The visible Share Event route is gated away even though sharing is always available.** Event Detail's overflow menu always contains Share Event and Copy Event Details (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:783-795`), but the toolbar presents that menu only when the user can edit the call window or has unpublished schedule changes (`:250-252`, `:776-780`). Students and staff without those edit states can therefore reach the activity view only by discovering a long press on the event card (`:1122-1136`, `:1224-1226`). Keep a standard Share button or the always-useful overflow visible for every role that can view the event; retain long press only as a shortcut.
- [x] **Items models a mutually exclusive sort choice as a command menu.** `ItemSortMenu` builds ordinary Buttons and draws the current checkmark itself even though choosing Popular, Name, Category, or Status changes one persistent selection rather than issuing a command (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:901-929`). Use a Picker-backed pop-up button, as Guides and Bookings already do, so the platform owns single-selection semantics and announcement.
- [x] **Some conditional pull-down buttons collapse to only one or two commands.** Schedule's More menu contains only Shift Calendar for users who cannot manage availability and only two destinations for those who can (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:1119-1139`); Item Detail can likewise show More for only Copy QR Code or Open Product Link because the trigger requires merely one eligible action (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemDetailView.swift:22-26`, `:102-141`). Apple's guidance recommends at least three menu items and direct controls for one or two actions. Promote the lone/common action or conditionally replace the menu with a labeled button while retaining overflow only when it adds real grouping value.
- [x] **Several action-sheet titles can exceed Apple's single-line target.** The shell uses the full sentence “You already have a reservation in progress” (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AppTabView.swift:176-194`), and passkey removal, shift assignment/replacement, and Trade Board claiming interpolate user-controlled names directly into titles (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AccountSecuritySettingsView.swift:285-297`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Schedule/AssignStudentSheet.swift:298-302`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Schedule/TradeBoardSheet.swift:464-481`). Keep the title to the short action or question and move variable identity/context into the optional message so compact-width and accessibility-size presentations remain quick to scan.
- [x] **The kiosk's landscape full-screen lock has a known platform deadline.** The dedicated mounted-iPad target deliberately pairs landscape-only orientations with `UIRequiresFullScreen` (`ios/project.yml:114-130`, `ios/Wisconsin/KioskOnly/Info.plist:38-49`). This preserves the accepted counter-appliance behavior today, and its source already retains a 640×540 minimum plus adaptive scan/rail layouts for a future windowed scene (`ios/Wisconsin/KioskOnly/KioskOnlyApp.swift:19-31`, `ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskChrome.swift:61-100`). Xcode 26 warns that the full-screen key will be ignored in a future release, so portrait or resized presentation will eventually escape the current physical-device acceptance boundary. Keep the present mounted contract, but complete the tracked managed-device windowing migration before Apple removes enforcement (`docs/GAPS_AND_RISKS.md:68`).
- [x] **Scene or process recreation returns the main app to Home instead of restoring navigation context.** The scene owns a fresh in-memory `AppState` (`ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:9-16`), whose selected tab defaults to Home (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/AppState.swift:23-40`), while Home, Items, Guides, Browse, Bookings, Schedule, Search, and Users keep their navigation paths only in local `@State` values. No `@SceneStorage`, restoration activity, or data-bound scene value restores the selected tab and pushed destination after the system discards and recreates the scene. Active reservation work is protected separately by background draft autosave, so this is a nonblocking loss of place rather than data loss. Persist a bounded, account-safe scene route and restore only destinations still allowed for the signed-in user.
- [x] **Initials in the smallest avatar fallbacks can render below Apple's 11-point text minimum and do not scale with Dynamic Type.** The shared app avatar fixes initials to `max(size * 0.36, 9)` (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/UserAvatarView.swift:34-41`), so the 28-point crew avatar renders them at about 10 points (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:1909`). The kiosk avatar similarly fixes initials to `size * 0.4` (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskComponents.swift:956-990`), while roster metrics can reduce the avatar to 26 points (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskIdleRoster.swift:48-49`, `:140-149`), yielding about 10.4-point text. Full names remain adjacent and in accessibility labels, so this is nonblocking; clamp the visual initials to at least 11 points and scale that clamp relative to an appropriate text style.
- [x] **A few prompts speak as “we” or expose implementation language instead of staying person- and task-centered.** Push and camera pre-prompts say “We'll only ping you” and “we'll jump straight to its record” (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/PushPrePromptView.swift:24-38`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScanPrePromptView.swift:30-44`); registration and password recovery likewise use “we” (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NativeAuthViews.swift:68`, `:171`, `:236`). Notification and kiosk recovery copy mentions a “notification server,” reaching “the server,” and a changed “server response” (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NotificationSettingsView.swift:190-207`, `ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskIdleView.swift:381-423`, `ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskOperatorHubView.swift:696-709`). Replace these with direct outcomes and next steps such as what notifications cover, what the scan opens, or when to check the connection and retry.
- [x] **The system launch screen is explicitly used as a branding opportunity.** The main target configures `LaunchLockup` as the system-owned launch image (`ios/project.yml:48-51`, `ios/Wisconsin/Supporting/Info.plist:48-56`), and the focused contract deliberately requires a branded first frame (`tests/ios-launch-experience.test.ts:23-33`). SwiftUI then continues the same centered mascot-and-wordmark lockup while a session actually restores (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/LaunchView.swift:54-88`, `:106-142`). There is no artificial minimum duration, delayed progress becomes truthful recovery information, and the sign-in use is appropriate, so this is nonblocking; simplify the system frame to a content-neutral continuity background and reserve the full lockup for sign-in or onboarding if strict Branding guidance is the goal.
- [x] **Eleven live accent-color calls bypass the app's established Wisconsin-red token.** The root scene applies `.tint(.brandPrimary)`, but the asset catalog provides no `AccentColor`; direct `Color.accentColor` references therefore remain system blue, an inconsistency already documented in the design language. They still color push and camera pre-prompts (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/PushPrePromptView.swift:20`, `:73`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScanPrePromptView.swift:26`, `:72`), link-sticker progress (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/DevTools/LinkStickerWizard.swift:87`, `:106`), account and user avatar fallbacks (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AccountAvatar.swift:14-15`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/UserAvatarView.swift:9-10`), and informational banner actions (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Components/BannerView.swift:45`). Choose the brand token where these are identity/accent roles or explicitly choose a semantic blue where blue carries meaning; avoid leaving the outcome dependent on a missing catalog asset.
- [x] **The kiosk's primary checkout action uses the noun as a verb.** “Checkout Gear” appears as the action title and inside supporting instructions (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskOperatorHubView.swift:234-239`, `:284-291`, `:367-386`). Elsewhere the product correctly uses “checkout” for the custody record and “check out” for the action. Rename this command to “Check Out Gear” and update its supporting sentences so the distinction remains consistent.
- [x] **Comparable recovery actions use three labels and two capitalization patterns.** List and regional failures generally use “Retry,” while event and Scoreboard errors use “Try Again,” and reservation, search, and Welcome failures use “Try again” (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/EventDetailSheet.swift:338-346`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/TeamScoreboardView.swift:142-149`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/CreateBookingSheet.swift:138-150`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/GlobalSearchSheet.swift:265-275`, `ios/Wisconsin/Views/Welcome/ProfileCompletionWelcomeComponents.swift:568-579`). Choose one concise recovery pattern per component type and apply its capitalization consistently.
- [x] **Camera permission copy is clear in intent but misses Apple's purpose-string and pre-alert conventions.** Both targets use the fragment “Scan gear barcodes and QR codes” without a subject or ending period (`ios/project.yml:61`, `:131`), while Apple recommends a brief, complete, active sentence. The main scanner's otherwise compliant one-action pre-alert labels its button “Turn on camera” (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScanPrePromptView.swift:48-57`), which resembles the system decision rather than clarifying that the next step opens Apple's alert. Use a sentence such as “Wisconsin Creative uses the camera to scan gear barcodes and QR codes.” and title the pre-alert action Continue or Next.
- [x] **The first notification soft prompt is scheduled automatically after sign-in instead of following demonstrated interest.** For every eligible account with undetermined notification authorization, the root scene waits only 600 milliseconds after the Login-to-app transition and presents the prompt (`ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:657-685`). The custom sheet does preserve choice and the system alert appears only after a deliberate tap (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/PushPrePromptView.swift:42-59`), so this is not coercive or blocking; moving first exposure to a notification-relevant action or the existing Profile/Settings entry would better match Apple's guidance to ask when the benefit is contextual and expected.
- [x] **Every ordinary APNs alert falls through to the same default Active interruption level.** The shared sender defines title, body, payload, and action category, but no `interruption-level` (`src/lib/push/apns.ts:376-403`). That makes a low-priority gear-prep nudge, a routine reservation update, and an imminent or overdue custody alert indistinguishable to Focus and scheduled delivery. Define a small server-owned urgency mapping, keep low-value informational work Passive or Active, and use Time Sensitive only for directly relevant events happening now or within the next hour; never derive urgency from arbitrary admin-authored copy.
- [x] **Foreground delivery always repeats the full system interruption.** While the app is active, every allowed notification is presented with banner, sound, and badge without checking whether the destination or equivalent inbox/banner content is already visible (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppDelegate.swift:107-119`). Keep the inbox and count refresh, but suppress or soften foreground presentation when the person is already viewing the affected booking, event, blast, or notifications surface.
- [x] **A local one-hour snooze is not reconciled with a later in-app pause or push opt-out.** Snooze creates a pending local request with default sound and a stable `gt-snooze-…` identifier (`ios/Wisconsin/App/NotificationActions.swift:93-141`), while native preference mutations only save server state and do not remove these pending requests (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Preferences.swift:57-125`). Sign-out correctly clears all pending notifications, but changing notification choice can still leave an already-scheduled reminder that fires afterward. Cancel pending snoozes when push delivery is paused or disabled, or explicitly separate chosen local reminders from the global push preference in the settings copy.
- [x] **Every registered notification category includes an action that merely opens the same destination as tapping the notification.** Booking offers “View Booking,” Schedule offers “View Shift,” and Blast offers “Open,” all through the shared `GT_VIEW` action (`ios/Wisconsin/App/NotificationActions.swift:25-59`, `:83-84`). The response handler deliberately makes that action and the default tap identical (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppDelegate.swift:173-181`). Remove the redundant buttons so the detail view stays focused on useful no-launch work such as Snooze and Got it; a tap already opens the related content.
- [x] **Notification categories provide no generic hidden-preview description.** All three categories are registered without `hiddenPreviewsBodyPlaceholder`, so when a person chooses to hide previews the system falls back to the generic “Notification” treatment instead of distinguishing a booking reminder, schedule update or operational message (`ios/Wisconsin/App/NotificationActions.swift:20-72`). Supply concise category-level placeholders that preserve useful category context in that user-selected presentation mode.
- [x] **Seven TipKit tips have no app-selected display cadence.** The app calls `Tips.configure()` without a display-frequency option (`ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:19-27`) and defines seven separate one-display tips (`:215-340`). Several become eligible close together—for example, Shift Calendar and My Availability both unlock after the third Schedule visit—so completion or dismissal can expose another tip immediately instead of giving the person time to use what they just learned. Configure a reasonable shared cadence, such as daily, while retaining the event rules and one-display limits; reserve any frequency override for genuinely immediate recovery guidance.
- [x] **The first Schedule tip describes actions that aren’t available to every person who receives it.** `ScheduleOpenWorkTip` tells every internal Schedule viewer to “Claim an open shift, post a trade, or review your availability,” and has no audience or event rule beyond its one-display cap (`ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:243-255`). The tip is attached to the Trade Board button for every non-collaborator, but My Availability exists only when `staffingType == "ST"` and lives in the separate More menu (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:897-943`, `:1094-1137`). Keep this control-specific tip about the Trade Board action it points to, or add role/capability eligibility and separate the availability discovery into its already-targeted tip.
- [x] **Selecting one day in the checkout trend creates the same inspection marks once for every point in the series.** The `RuleMark` and `PointMark` depend only on `selectedDay`, but they are declared inside `Chart(points)`' per-point content closure (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ReportsView.swift:311-347`). A 30-day series therefore stacks 30 identical rules and points whenever someone inspects a day, adding redundant chart content and potentially repetitive mark-level accessibility output. Build the trend marks from the data loop and add the selected rule/point once outside that loop; retain the existing selected-day text callout and default Swift Charts Audio Graph support.
- [x] **Two kiosk informational content surfaces use Liquid Glass instead of the content-layer surface system.** `KioskScanStage` applies `.glassEffect(.regular)` to the entire large scan/confirmation stage, including its headline, instructions, accepted-item state, and embedded actions (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskComponents.swift:260-315`). The release-mode scanner-status capsule is likewise an informational `Label`, not a control, but receives the same effect (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskIdentityView.swift:266-293`). Apple's Materials guidance reserves Liquid Glass for the functional control and navigation layer and recommends standard materials for content. Replace these containers with the existing `kioskCard`/`KioskSurface` content treatment while retaining glass on their actual camera, help, back, and choice controls. This is a visual-hierarchy refinement; the scan workflows and status communication remain usable.
- [x] **The full-screen photo viewer's double-tap zoom ignores Reduce Motion.** Double-tapping always springs the image between 1× and 2.5× scale, and `ZoomableImageViewer` does not read the system preference (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Brand.swift:422-452`). Pinch remains a directly tracked gesture and should stay direct; make the double-tap change instantaneous or use a non-scaling alternative when Reduce Motion is enabled. This viewer is optional and the close control stays immediately available, so the refinement is nonblocking.
- [x] **The full-screen photo viewer offers zoom only through two touch gestures.** Pinch and double-tap are familiar and responsive, but the image exposes no visible zoom controls, keyboard commands, `accessibilityAdjustableAction`, named accessibility actions or equivalent route for VoiceOver, Switch Control and Voice Control (`ios/Wisconsin/Core/Brand.swift:422-452`). Viewing the fitted photo and closing remain available, so this is nonblocking; add a compact zoom-in, zoom-out and reset treatment or equivalent adjustable accessibility contract while preserving direct pinch and double-tap as shortcuts.
- [x] **Schedule's custom month calendar is not ready for an RTL localization.** The project currently declares only Base and English and contains no String Catalog (`ios/Wisconsin.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:922-928`), so this does not break the shipped English-only interface. If Arabic or Hebrew is added, however, the calendar still supplies fixed English Sunday-through-Saturday initials and spoken names (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScheduleView.swift:1871-1903`, `:1954-1956`), computes every month as Sunday-first instead of respecting `Calendar.current.firstWeekday` (`:2105-2112`), and maps a physical rightward drag to the previous month without consulting `layoutDirection` (`:1935-1941`). Derive weekday order and labels from the active Calendar/Locale and reverse the custom page gesture by semantic previous/next direction before declaring RTL support.
