Protected-resource use remains narrow: the main app and kiosk request camera access only inside an opened scanning or camera workflow, notification authorization reaches the system alert only after a deliberate tap, and profile photos use the system `PhotosPicker` without broad library authorization. No location, microphone, contacts, calendar, health, Bluetooth, local-network, advertising-identifier, or tracking request exists. Denied camera access exposes typed-code alternatives and Settings recovery; unsupported scanning keeps hand-scanner or typed entry available; denied notifications remain visible and recoverable in native Settings; existing authorization is read before registration; and account/session guards prevent delayed notification work crossing users. The two shared P2 refinements remain: both targets' camera purpose string is a fragment without a subject or period, and the main camera pre-alert says “Turn on camera” instead of clearly naming the handoff to Apple's alert (`ios/project.yml:61`, `:131`; `ios/Wisconsin/Views/ScanPrePromptView.swift:48-57`); the first notification education sheet still appears automatically 600 milliseconds after sign-in rather than after demonstrated interest (`ios/Wisconsin/App/WisconsinApp.swift:657-685`). No new P0/P1/P2 finding was added. Physical camera and notification prompts, denial/revocation, Settings return, VoiceOver, interruption, reinstall/reset behavior, APNs delivery, and compiled purpose-string read-back remain runtime gates.
- **Searching:** Search has an appropriate primary position as a dedicated, one-tap system search tab and opens with keyboard focus. Its role-aware prompt clearly narrows collaborators to reservable gear while authorized staff search items, reservations, check-outs, and people concurrently; a 300-millisecond debounce and stale-response guards keep live results responsive and correct. Results are grouped by source, partial endpoint failures stay visible without discarding successful groups, complete failure offers recovery, and asset, booking, and person rows lead to their destinations. Camera lookup is available as an alternate input, and local searches remain close to distinct Items, Bookings, Users, Guides, event, requester, and equipment tasks. Spotlight indexing is deliberately private and bounded to the signed-in person's active bookings, replaces stale domain content, expires after return, and clears at the session boundary. Two P1s block the pass: every global source is silently capped at ten with totals discarded and no path to the rest, and bulk-item family matches—including successful scans—land on noninteractive rows instead of a destination or Reserve action. The existing P2 remains: recent searches are shown only to collaborators and are recorded only on keyboard submission, not when a live result is opened. Physical keyboard, VoiceOver, Dynamic Type, slow/partial/offline response, large-result pagination, result ordering, scanning, deep-link authorization, Spotlight lifecycle, and cross-account privacy remain runtime gates.
- **Settings:** the custom hub is intentionally small and uses sensible defaults. Theme follows the system unless a person deliberately chooses a device-local override; account, notification, privacy, support, version, system-permission, sign-out, and Admin-only presentation controls are grouped in an inset system list with role-aware visibility and accessibility-size reflow. Task-specific Schedule, booking, guide, report, scoreboard, user, availability, and reservation choices stay in the screens they affect, while camera and notification authorization stay system-owned with a direct iOS Settings link. The main app exposes no redundant accessibility, authentication, scrolling, language, region, network, controller, or performance settings, and no `Settings.bundle` is needed for its rarely changed choices. The two notification P1s are closed: delivery status now exposes durable pause and full registration truth, and normal preference-save failures visibly and audibly recover after reverting. Four P2 refinements apply: the staged Sign out other devices command looks like a persistent toggle, app-authored haptics have no device-local mute, Preview as Student draws a disclosure chevron for an immediate mode switch, and the Home-only Profile route has no Command-Comma keyboard equivalent. No separate finding applies to the deliberate Light/Dark override because System remains the default and Apple explicitly allows an app-level interface-style choice when useful. Persistence across launch, sign-out, account switching, appearance changes, OS permission return, offline save/retry, simultaneous toggles, role-preview transition, VoiceOver, Dynamic Type, Switch Control, and physical-keyboard access remain runtime gates.
- **Undo and redo:** ordinary SwiftUI and UIKit text fields retain the system editing stack, and the app does not redefine the three-finger swipe or shake gesture. Focused creation and editing flows generally protect unsaved work with drafts, Cancel/Discard/Keep Editing choices, or failure-time optimistic restoration; operational custody and lifecycle mutations use confirmations, audit history, or explicit compensating actions rather than pretending a hidden local undo can reverse server truth. Schedule's private working copy now stores named per-command snapshots, exposes server-backed Undo/Redo buttons, connects those commands to the system UndoManager and Command-Z/Shift-Command-Z, and retains the named all-change Revert. The former Schedule P1 is closed locally. Two P2 refinements apply: a successful Mark All Read has no mark-unread or Undo route, and Reset crop cannot restore the prior adjustment state. Runtime proof remains open for standard text undo, named result feedback, offscreen-result revelation, three-finger and shake behavior, physical-keyboard shortcuts, concurrent Schedule versions, mark-all recovery, crop Reset, accessibility, and the kiosk's custom UIKit fields.
- **Workouts:** Wisconsin Creative is an athletics operations product, not a health or fitness tracker. Neither native application imports HealthKit or WorkoutKit, starts or saves an `HKWorkoutSession`, reads sensors or fitness metrics, declares HealthKit entitlements or health-purpose strings, enables workout background processing, offers workout controls or summaries, or ships a watchOS application (`ios/project.yml:31-217`; `ios/Wisconsin/Wisconsin.entitlements`; `ios/Wisconsin/Supporting/Info.plist`). This is the privacy-preserving result Apple asks for when an app doesn't provide health and fitness functionality. The checkout-return Live Activity uses ActivityKit only for operational gear-return state and a booking deep link; its paired-watch Smart Stack layout remains a glanceable extension of that same custody reminder, not a workout (`ios/Wisconsin/LiveActivities/CheckoutReturnActivityAttributes.swift:1-88`; `ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/CheckoutReturnLiveActivityWidget.swift:1-260`). The kiosk's single blue/green ring communicates a labeled scanned-item count and completion state, not Move, Exercise, or Stand progress (`ios/Wisconsin/Kiosk/KioskComponents.swift:613-646`). No workout catalog, start/pause/resume/end controls, live fitness metrics, sensor-unavailable explanation, session summary, accidental short-session handling, or Activity rings are needed in the current product, and no P0/P1/P2 finding applies. Revisit this pass before adding any health/fitness feature, workout intent, HealthKit access, workout background mode, or fitness-styled ring.
- **Action button:** the app exposes six focused App Shortcuts instead of a redundant generic Open App action. Scan Code and Reserve Gear open the exact interactive task; What's Out and Next Shift use locally authenticated Siri/Shortcuts snippets to answer without leaving the current context; My Gear and Schedule open their specific operational destinations. The background answers request only the signed-in person's gear or shift data, show signed-out and humanized API failures, and use compact, accessible summaries (`ios/Wisconsin/App/AppIntentsData.swift:1-243`). Open Booking separately requires local device authentication, and none of the actions performs checkout, return, extension, assignment, or another custody mutation from the hardware button (`ios/Wisconsin/App/BookingEntity.swift:1-85`; `ios/Wisconsin/App/AppIntents.swift:46-92`). The system owns Action button configuration; the app adds no duplicate tutorial or custom hardware-press listener, and a visible in-app path remains available for every shortcut task. One P1 blocks the pass: the static App Shortcuts catalog isn't role-adaptive, so a collaborator can configure an unavailable action that the app silently declines or consumes without a result. One P2 applies: four configuration labels aren't verb-led. Physical iPhone Action button configuration, locked/unlocked authentication, Siri/Spotlight parity, cold/warm launch routing, offline and expired-session feedback, VoiceOver, and current-context snippet presentation remain runtime gates.
- **Apple Pencil and Scribble:** the accepted signature workflow is authenticated iPad Safari, not a native PencilKit canvas. Its pen-class Pointer Events contract begins a visible stroke on contact, consumes coalesced and terminal samples, preserves interrupted ink, uses stable logical coordinates across resize and rotation, and keeps touch reserved for clearly separate 44-point controls (`src/app/(app)/signatures/[id]/capture/[memberId]/SignatureCapturePage.tsx:343-429`, `:563-629`; `src/lib/signatures/pointer.ts:1-30`). The layout keeps drawing controls above the canvas and commit controls below it instead of pinning them beneath either writing hand; Undo and Redo operate one stroke at a time, and Clear is itself undoable. The surface offers one fixed pen rather than a tool picker, so it neither overrides the person's double-tap/squeeze settings nor assigns barrel roll or hover a destructive action. Uniform width is an explicit output contract for consistent production assets, so pressure, tilt, azimuth, taper, and barrel-roll variation remain deliberately out of scope rather than missing brush behavior (`docs/AREA_SIGNATURES.md:22`). One shared P1 blocks the pass: the kiosk's custom `UITextField` delegate can misclassify a multi-character Scribble transcription as scanner input and reject it. PencilKit remains intentionally deferred under D-050 (`docs/GAPS_AND_RISKS.md:48`); replacing the accepted web workflow is not the repair. A real Pencil save, Pencil-as-control taps, finger/palm rejection, left- and right-handed use, rotation, interruption, hover behavior, standard text-field Scribble, kiosk Scribble/scanner separation, VoiceOver, and authenticated artifact delivery remain physical-iPad runtime gates.
- **Camera Control:** Wisconsin Creative uses an iPhone camera as an authenticated barcode-reading sensor, not as a photo or video capture experience. The main scanner delegates recognition, highlighting, and guidance to VisionKit's `DataScannerViewController`, exposes only task-relevant Close, flashlight, typed-code, status, and recovery controls, and sends the recognized value into role- and session-bound gear lookup (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/QRScannerSheet.swift:35-175`, `:305-424`). It does not import LockedCameraCapture, install an `AVCaptureEventInteraction`, register `AVCaptureControl` sliders or pickers, provide a shutter, adjust zoom/exposure/filter values, or define a locked-camera extension; the only app extension is the existing WidgetKit Live Activity target (`ios/project.yml:31-96`, `:195-225`). That non-adoption is appropriate: launching authenticated inventory lookup from a locked camera surface would not capture user media and would cross the product's session and custody boundaries. Scan already has a visible in-app route and an App Shortcut, while Camera Control remains available for the system Camera app (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/Search/GlobalSearchSheet.swift:162-192`; `ios/Wisconsin/App/AppIntents.swift:94-104`). The dedicated kiosk is iPad-only, and Apple does not support Camera Control on iPadOS, so its camera fallback is outside this pass. Because Wisconsin adds no Camera Control overlay, the overlay-specific symbol, short-name, value-unit, prominent-value, ordering, mode-enablement, duplication, and viewfinder-clearance rules do not currently apply. No P0/P1/P2 finding applies; reassess before adding capture controls, hardware shutter handling, or a locked-camera extension.
- **Digital Crown:** Apple supports app-directed Crown input on watchOS and reserves the Apple Vision Pro Crown for system interactions; the guidance is explicitly unsupported on iOS and iPadOS. Wisconsin Creative declares iOS application, test, UI-test, kiosk, and WidgetKit extension targets only, all against the iPhone/iPad SDK (`ios/project.yml:31-225`). Source and generated project contain no watchOS or visionOS product, WatchKit interface, `digitalCrownRotation`, crown sequencer, custom Crown haptics, immersive space, or other Crown consumer. The checkout-return Live Activity does render a `.small` Apple Watch Smart Stack layout, but it is supplied by the existing iOS WidgetKit extension and selects only an ActivityKit presentation family (`ios/WisconsinLiveActivities/CheckoutReturnLiveActivityWidget.swift:176-179`, `:224-239`); turning the Crown navigates the system-owned Smart Stack rather than sending rotation data to Wisconsin Creative. Consequently, Crown-anchored vertical navigation, touch equivalents, data inspection, visual feedback, speed tracking, and detent tuning have no app-owned surface to evaluate. The icon package's watchOS circle declaration is reusable artwork metadata, not a watch target. No P0/P1/P2 finding applies; reopen this pass before adding a watchOS app or any watch-native interactive surface.
- **Eyes:** Apple defines eyes as a visionOS indirect-input channel: people look to identify an interactive object, the system provides private hover feedback without exposing gaze before selection, and a separate gesture activates it. Wisconsin Creative declares iOS application, test, UI-test, kiosk, and WidgetKit extension targets only, all against the iPhone/iPad SDK (`ios/project.yml:31-225`). Source and generated project contain no visionOS product, spatial window or volume, RealityKit or ARKit eye-input session, gaze query, custom spatial hover effect, Look to Scroll integration, spatial tap gesture, or other gaze consumer. The absence of an app-owned eye-tracking path is the privacy-preserving result for this product, and iPad pointer behavior belongs to the separate Pointing devices pass. Consequently, gaze-target spacing, hover shapes and crowding, gaze-versus-focus feedback, indirect activation, alternate input, visual comfort, and destructive gaze-only action rules have no app-owned surface to evaluate. No P0/P1/P2 finding applies; reopen this pass before adding a visionOS app, spatial interface, gaze-driven interaction, or custom visionOS hover treatment.
- **Focus and selection:** the native products rely on standard SwiftUI controls and system list, form, search, tab, menu, picker, and text-entry behavior; source contains no custom `UIFocus` graph, disabled system focus effect, free-form pointer substitute, tvOS target, or visionOS target. Explicit `FocusState` use is bounded to form progression, search/manual-code entry, keyboard dismissal, validation recovery, and scanner-versus-editor ownership, while selected tiles and filter rows generally pair visible state with `.isSelected`. Two shared P1s block the pass. First, changing an iPad window from regular to compact width removes the four sidebar-only destinations and programmatically changes any active one to Home, discarding both selection and context without the person's interaction (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/AppTabView.swift:25-42`, `:84-130`; `tests/ios-tabbar-stability.test.ts:71-78`). Second, invited registration locks the preapproved email but routes Full name's Next action into that disabled field instead of Password (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/NativeAuthViews.swift:49-60`, `:72-91`). Two existing P2 refinements also apply: the Items status filter and mutually exclusive sort menu draw checkmarks inside ordinary Buttons rather than letting native Toggle or Picker selection semantics own state and announcement (`ios/Wisconsin/Views/ItemsView.swift:852-929`). ## Acceptance and known limits

- [x] Current source inspected for all recorded component passes.
- [x] Focused source-contract suites passed as recorded above.

- [x] Brand marks, naming, voice, accent roles, custom type roles, launch/sign-in placement, and authenticated-logo restraint inspected.
- [x] Immersive-experience platform scope confirmed as unsupported on iOS/iPadOS; no visionOS target or immersive-only API is present, and camera scanners remain bounded 2D capture surfaces.
- [x] Inclusion source coverage inspected across identity assumptions, person imagery, apparel context, onboarding approachability, welcoming language, accessibility, and declared localization scope.
- [x] Privacy source coverage inspected across permission timing, usage descriptions, manifests, tracking declarations, authentication storage, local caches, widgets, Spotlight, account cleanup, and deletion access.
- [x] Chart purpose, chart type, scale, axes, period context, labels, color independence, interaction, adjacent values, and source-level accessibility representation inspected across Reports and Scoreboard.
- [x] Charts component source scope inspected against Apple's current anatomy, mark, axis, descriptive-content, hierarchy, compact-layout, interaction, change-communication, color and accessibility guidance. Reports contains the only three Swift Charts: a line-and-area daily checkout trend with touch selection and a persistent textual value callout, an asset-status sector chart followed by a complete labeled count list, and a horizontal custody-days ranking with category and value axes. Scoreboard's two custom proportion displays are deliberately small explanatory marks: the season meter has one complete record-and-rate accessibility label and a hidden visual key, while each breakdown bar is hidden beneath a row that names the category, record, game count and win rate. The forms match their questions, bars retain meaningful zero baselines, automatic ranges let variable operational totals use the plot area, titles and surrounding copy establish purpose, semantic fills adapt across appearance, and no critical value depends on color or chart interaction alone. Swift Charts supplies mark-level accessibility and Audio Graphs; the custom marks supply concise grouped alternatives. The one existing P2 remains: Reports declares the selected `RuleMark` and `PointMark` inside `Chart(points)`' per-point closure, stacking the same inspection pair once per datum. Move that pair outside the data loop while preserving the current callout and selection behavior. No P0/P1 chart blocker applies; Audio Graph summaries, VoiceOver order, keyboard and Switch Control navigation, Dynamic Type, appearance, selection, animation alternatives, and representative sparse/dense datasets remain runtime gates.
- [x] Web-views component source scope inspected across embedded HTML and website presentation, WebKit declarations, navigation ownership, browser imitation, external handoffs, authentication, deep links, accessibility, recovery and platform support. The current native products contain no `WKWebView`, WebKit import, `SFSafariViewController`, SafariServices dependency, embedded HTML renderer, browser toolbar, URL field, history stack or web-navigation delegate. That omission is purposeful. Guides are first-class native SwiftUI pages backed by sanitized Markdown; inline links and labeled YouTube/Vimeo cards use system `Link` handoff, with media explicitly announced as opening in the browser. Privacy policy, support mail, product pages, profile management, license management and web-only sidebar destinations similarly leave through system-owned handlers, while app and iOS Settings routes use their platform URL schemes. The Schedule subscription handoff opens its generated `webcal` URL and reports failure instead of embedding a browser. Passkeys use `ASAuthorizationController` and the shared webcredentials association, not a web view. Because no current app-owned surface traverses multiple web pages, forward/back web controls are not missing, and avoiding a miniature Safari preserves native context, cookies, accessibility, content blocking and browser ownership. No P0/P1/P2 finding applies. Reopen before embedding any remote page or HTML document; first justify why native content or a system browser handoff is insufficient, then require destination allowlisting, navigation policy, forward/back controls for multipage paths, loading and failure recovery, privacy and cookie boundaries, download and external-scheme handling, accessibility and process-crash recovery without growing a general-purpose browser.
- [x] Boxes component source scope inspected across logical grouping, container scale, nested-box restraint, padding, alignment, titles, adaptive backgrounds, borders, accessibility and platform behavior. The current native products contain no SwiftUI `GroupBox`, custom `GroupBoxStyle`, AppKit `NSBox` or box-specific title contract. Their closest equivalents are purpose-built small card surfaces. The main app's shared `brandCard` adds bounded padding, a continuous rounded secondary grouped background and a half-point separator hairline; `cardSurfaceRaised` uses the system tertiary grouped background only for subordinate tiles or controls. The kiosk's `kioskCard` similarly centralizes a restrained fill, glaze and stroke for individual counter tasks, rows and status tiles, while the compact Mac companion uses a few local rounded status panels rather than a box hierarchy. Representative Home, Items, Bookings, Guides, Licenses, Schedule, Scoreboard, event, checkout and kiosk compositions keep cards materially smaller than their screen or sheet. They communicate internal subgroups through headings, spacing, aligned rows, dividers, labels and sunken input controls instead of nesting another full card boundary around each subgroup. Section headings and accessible row/card labels supply relationship context where a visible title adds value, while concise cards whose contents already name themselves avoid redundant titles. This is the visual distinction Apple asks for without turning every grouping into a heavy box. No P0/P1/P2 finding applies; reopen before adding `GroupBox`/`NSBox`, a full-screen bordered container, or recursive card composition, and validate whether spacing or alignment can communicate the inner relationship more clearly.
- [x] Column-views component source scope inspected across Apple’s macOS-only hierarchical browser pattern, deep parent-child navigation, root and terminal presentation, selection, resizable widths, long labels, accessibility, and platform boundaries. The repository contains no AppKit `NSBrowser`, browser delegate, browser cell, or equivalent app-authored hierarchy of adjacent columns. The main app’s regular-width `.sidebarAdaptable` `TabView` exposes top-level destinations rather than successive levels of one data tree; Browse uses a `NavigationStack` and native list of directory links. The kiosk’s scan/content rails and side-by-side checkout form, event, control, and manifest regions are parallel operational workspaces that stack when space or Dynamic Type requires it, not parent selections that reveal child columns. The native Mac companion is a compact `MenuBarExtra` with a fixed-size Settings window, not a document or hierarchy browser. Apple explicitly directs iPadOS hierarchy presentation toward split views and does not support Column views on iOS or iPadOS, so no speculative browser is required. No P0/P1/P2 finding applies. Reopen before adding a deep macOS hierarchy browser; then start at the root, expose each selected parent’s children in the next column, consider a terminal preview, let people resize columns, preserve selection and keyboard/VoiceOver context, and keep an appropriate split or stack fallback on iPadOS.
- [x] Lockups component source scope inspected across Apple’s tvOS-only focus unit, content/header/footer composition, spacing for focused expansion, consistent row and group sizing, cards, caption buttons, monograms, posters, media context, tilt behavior, accessibility and platform boundaries. Wisconsin Creative declares iOS applications and tests, an iOS WidgetKit extension, and a separate native macOS application; it has no tvOS application or extension, TVUIKit dependency, `TVLockupView`, `TVLockupHeaderFooterView`, `TVCardView`, `TVCaptionButtonView`, `TVMonogramContentView`, `TVPosterView`, tvOS focus graph, living-room media catalog, cast or crew browser, rating/review card row, or poster shelf. - [x] Outline-views component source scope inspected across Apple’s macOS hierarchical table pattern, primary and supplemental columns, disclosure, headings, per-level sorting, resizing, expansion persistence, alternating rows, editing, reordering, truncation, search, accessibility and platform boundaries. - [x] Designing-for-macOS source scope inspected across the separate native Wisconsin Creative menu-bar companion and its deliberate handoff to the web control room; large and multiple displays; stationary desk use and one-to-three-foot viewing distance; physical keyboard, pointer, Siri and mixed-input applicability; quick checks, prolonged operational work and active/inactive app transitions; menu-bar-extra, Settings-window, Dock, full-screen and file-management boundaries; compact information density and modal restraint; menu access, keyboard shortcuts, precise selection, hover and focus; user-controlled launch, count and notification preferences; appearance, accessibility, privacy, interruption, recovery, deep links, signing and distribution. The menu-bar companion is intentionally a glanceable, read-only status and notification utility rather than the hours-long desktop editor, so its anchored popover, content-sized Settings window, absence from the Dock, lack of document and full-screen workflows, and browser handoff are purposeful utility-app boundaries rather than platform defects. Current source has no P0/P1/P2 finding; the active companion plan continues to own installed cold-restart, interaction, accessibility, password-manager and real-APNs acceptance.
- [x] Designing-for-tvOS source scope inspected across living-room product fit; authoritative iOS, iPad kiosk and native macOS target declarations; very large high-resolution display and eight-foot-plus viewing distance; stationary and moving-room ergonomics; Siri Remote, game controller, voice and other-device input; long-form immersion and Picture in Picture; TV app, SharePlay, Top Shelf and TV-provider integration; focus-system navigation and state; cinematic edge-to-edge artwork, animation and audio; shared sign-in, profiles and current-viewer switching; privacy, accessibility, interruption and recovery; external Guide-video handoff; and the kiosk's narrowly scoped `GCKeyboard` barcode-scanner signal. Wisconsin Creative is an operational gear, scheduling, custody and game-day handoff system with no living-room media, gaming, fitness, education or home-utility requirement. The repository declares no tvOS application or extension, TV Services or provider capability, Siri Remote or game-controller handler, tvOS focus graph, media player, Top Shelf surface, shared-TV profile model or TV-app integration. Guide videos open an external provider-owned player, and the iPad kiosk's GameController import observes only whether a Bluetooth HID scanner is present. Omitting a speculative Apple TV product is therefore aligned with the North Star rather than a missing workflow; no P0/P1/P2 finding applies.
- [x] Designing-for-visionOS source scope inspected across spatial-computing product fit; authoritative iOS, iPad kiosk and native macOS target declarations; infinite three-dimensional space; Shared Space windows, movable content, volumes and Full Space; progressive immersion, passthrough and Digital Crown ownership; Spatial Audio; eye targeting with indirect gestures and hand-based direct gestures; head-relative placement, field of view, stationary posture, reach duration, motion, visual comfort, physical safety and Apple's age context; VoiceOver, Switch Control, Dwell Control, Guided Access and Head Pointer; familiar windowed UI, minimum necessary immersion; SharePlay and spatial Personas; privacy, interruption, recovery and distribution; and the conventional two-dimensional WindowGroup, VisionKit scanners, external Guide video and Event Share boundaries. Wisconsin Creative has no approved spatial-computing workflow or visionOS target. Source contains no RealityKit, immersive-space, volume, 3D-model, passthrough, world- or hand-tracking, gaze, spatial-input, Spatial Audio or spatial-Persona implementation. The mobile and kiosk camera surfaces scan flat barcodes through system VisionKit, ordinary sharing exports bounded event text, and the current operational tasks already belong to handheld iOS, the staffed iPad counter, the macOS status companion or the web control room. Omitting a speculative headset product is therefore aligned with purpose, comfort and safety; no P0/P1/P2 finding applies.
- [x] Designing-for-watchOS source scope inspected across wrist-scale display and ergonomics; Always On; Digital Crown, touch, gestures, Action button, shortcuts and sensor inputs; sub-minute glance interactions; shallow single-screen hierarchy; complications, notifications and watch faces; app independence; target declarations; content visibility, accessibility, interruption and recovery; and the existing checkout-return Live Activity's supplemental `.small` family. Wisconsin Creative has no approved standalone watch app, and the repository correctly declares only iOS application and WidgetKit-extension targets. Its real watch-facing surface is the paired Apple Watch Smart Stack tile supplied by ActivityKit. That tile is concise, noninteractive, minute-aligned, stale-aware and focused on one return, so a speculative watch app, Crown handler, complication or watch-face feature is not required. Product direction explicitly permits its full booking title and timing. One P2 applies because the small-family presentation never reads `isLuminanceReduced`, leaving full-intensity urgency colors on the Always On wrist surface while its broad source test falsely claims dimming coverage. Pass and path-test reduced luminance through the Apple Watch presentation; do not add standalone watch scope without an approved wrist-native task.
- [x] Designing-for-games source scope inspected across game-product fit; installation and fast entry into play; launch, loading, defaults, onboarding, permission and review timing; display adaptation, typography, control sizes, graphics, safe areas, aspect ratios and full screen; each platform's default and alternate inputs; physical and touch game controls; perceivability, personalization, inclusive representation and stereotype review; Game Center, GameSave, Core Haptics, Spatial Audio and other optional Apple technologies; target declarations; and the operational Scoreboard, badges, accountability views and kiosk HID-scanner boundary. Wisconsin Creative is an athletics-operations product, not a game. It contains no gameplay loop, game engine, GameKit identity, Game Center capability, saved-game contract, game controller mapping, virtual controls, controller haptics, game audio, player avatars, narrative characters, multiplayer activity, player-submitted score or skill ranking. Scoreboard presents source-owned team results and work credits; badges recognize audited operational facts inside Wisconsin accounts; overdue and accountability rankings expose risk people should resolve, not competition they should prolong. The kiosk imports GameController only to observe whether a Bluetooth barcode scanner enumerates as `GCKeyboard`; camera and typed-code recovery remain available, and no gamepad input is read. Applying game onboarding, full-screen play, controller, avatar, Game Center or saved-progress conventions to these surfaces would misstate their purpose and weaken operational identity. No P0/P1/P2 finding applies; reopen before introducing actual gameplay, a game engine, gamepad interaction, player-created gaming identity, Game Center, game saves, game-specific haptics or immersive audio.

## Recommended bounded fix order

1. Replace self-service account deactivation with a truthful deletion/retention contract that erases or irreversibly pseudonymizes non-required personal and authentication data, then prove completion and partial-failure behavior.
2. Make native notification Settings represent and control durable pause and APNs registration truthfully, expose and announce save failures, and reconcile test-delivery and pending-snooze behavior.
3. Declare the main target's MetricKit file-timestamp required reason and add source-to-manifest coverage.
4. Connect the Apple Watch Live Activity's small-family palette to reduced luminance and make CarPlay an explicit co-owner of the small-family contract while preserving full booking and timing visibility.
5. Make global Search complete and actionable: preserve source totals, expose all matching results, and give every bulk-family match a visible destination or Reserve path.
6. Make every static App Shortcut universally meaningful or return an explicit role/sign-in failure, clear denied handoffs, and use verb-led Action button labels.
7. Give the private Schedule working copy concurrency-safe, named per-command undo and redo through system gestures and Command-Z while retaining the predictable all-change Revert.
8. Restore persistent tab navigation and remove the empty iOS 26.0 accessory artifact.
9. Preserve the active sidebar destination when an iPad window collapses to compact width, then restore an account-safe scene route after recreation.
10. Correct sheet stacking and modal dismissal semantics.
11. Restore the accessibility fundamentals: destination identity, interaction targets, kiosk identity scaling, registration focus, visible gesture alternatives, contrast, reduced motion, and transient-feedback announcements.
12. Restore standard Paste, text drag/drop, and Scribble in kiosk title fields by separating scanner-burst routing from ordinary `UITextField` edits.
13. Replace the two 96-value quarter-hour menus with bounded time selection that preserves the 15-minute contract.
14. Make account-setup requirements and validation visible while typing without enabling invalid submission.
15. Make profile-crop output an intentional 1024-pixel, bounded upload and test the real encoded result.
16. Replace the five nonexistent SF Symbol names and add catalog-backed dynamic-map coverage.
